# EXPANSION CW124-07 — The Storyteller — Stories in Our Hearts

## A prose-first memorial-story collection about public claims, private lives, and what a name cannot contain.

### Prose Wave 124: The Lives a Marker Cannot Contain

## Batch brief

**Content type:** original game-content proposal with scene, diegetic-record, conversation, and conditional-return drafts.
**Content bank:** 48 distinct beat pairings, each with four alternative authored forms (192 candidate passages).
**Current local anchor:** memorial_storyteller/stories_in_hearts — The Storyteller.
**Tone:** grounded, human, restrained, material, and careful about what a damaged record can prove.
**Scope:** game content only; no production code, JSON data, route, quest, flag, simulation, or save changes.

## 1. Expansion thesis

A prose-first narrative bank about retelling, selective memory, and changes that happen when stories pass between tired listeners. Proposed scenes, recollections, conversations, and returns give different readers room to respond to the memorial’s wording while preserving its unknowns. This is game content, not a feature specification, and adds no system or state.

## 2. Story question

How can a story about The Storyteller explore retelling, selective memory, and changes that happen when stories pass between tired listeners while keeping the catalog’s public claim separate from a complete life?

## 5. Human center

Stories are remembered differently, not magic that restores a community. Listeners may correct a fragment, forget an ending, recall a sound, or refuse to tell.

## 6. Voice and point of view

- **A listener recalling fragments:** Responds to The Storyteller’s words without claiming access to a complete biography.
- **A keeper of shared records:** Brings a present-day task or concern to the memorial, not an invented memory of the deceased.
- **A person who dislikes the tales:** Questions what the public wording leaves out without replacing it with another unsupported account.

Each speaker knows only what the cited source, their own proposed observation, or an explicitly attributed memory could give them. No proposed voice represents a whole faction or settlement.

## 7. Placement and current reachability

The source record verifies a content anchor, not a guaranteed playable route or passage consumer. Before selection, confirm current reachability, content schema, owner, and trigger. Candidate passages may be considered only through a verified existing consumer. Every bank entry remains a candidate, not a promise that all 48 beats occur.

## 8. Player agency

The player may read, ask, carry a sentence forward, leave it where it is, or decline to interpret it. These are editorial postures rather than a promised menu. A refusal or silence is a complete outcome. Any branch that needs runtime state must use the existing owner’s exposed state after verification.

## 9. Continuity, dignity, and safety

Do not add a physical grave, named survivor, relationship, exact chronology, private thought, or outcome beyond the source. Do not create a quest, flag, menu, reward, relationship delta, route, item, or save state. Treat the inscription as a public account with a limited point of view. Do not name or reproduce real-world stories, songs, characters, authors, or copied text. Do not invent a tale, audience, origin, lesson, supernatural effect, or sole ownership of identity. Do not make hazard, scarcity, illness, grief, technical uncertainty, institutional pressure, or memorial language into a puzzle tutorial. Keep proposed identity separate from authored identity and testimony separate from fact.

## 10. Existing hooks and implementation boundary

Candidate anchor: memorial_storyteller/stories_in_hearts, from Assets/StreamingAssets/Data/memorials_expansion_05.json. MemorialSystem owns survivor memorialization, grief, mourning, and eulogies, but consumption of this separate catalog record is unverified. Check the actual loader and narrative consumer before selecting prose.
The plan creates no parallel authority, new registry, save section, route graph, generic panel callback, or gameplay rule. A selected passage should attach only through a verified existing content owner.

## 11. Narrative sequence

The six movements below organize an editorial bank; they are not six required visits or a fixed quest chain. Beat order may change if the existing consumer calls for a shorter or non-linear presentation.

### Movement 1: Tales of the old world

The local memorial for The Storyteller places “tales of the old world” inside a compact public account. A reader can attend to retelling, selective memory, and changes that happen when stories pass between tired listeners while keeping the source’s claim separate from a whole biography. What can a later reader responsibly infer from “tales of the old world,” and what must remain unknown? The movement begins with retelling, selective memory, and changes that happen when stories pass between tired listeners through the phrase tales of the old world. It stays within the current source boundary and lets the people in the room disagree about meaning without creating a new rule.

## 12. Beat bank usage

Each of the 48 pairings below combines one movement with one local motif. The four forms are alternatives: a scene for an observable moment, a record for an attributable fragment, a conversation for disagreement, and a consequence vignette for a verified return condition. Selecting one does not select the others. The bank is deliberately larger than a single quest so writers can choose a truthful scale for the current consumer.

## 13. Player-facing tone

Keep sentences close to material detail. Let people speak in different rhythms: a keeper names what is present, a reader separates record from inference, and a passerby notices what the institution leaves outside its frame. Do not force a moral verdict. A person can decline, wait, misunderstand, or leave a sentence unfinished. The content should make room for a player’s attention instead of replacing it with a score.

## 14. Editorial acceptance

Do not add a physical grave, named survivor, relationship, exact chronology, private thought, or outcome beyond the source. Do not create a quest, flag, menu, reward, relationship delta, route, item, or save state. Treat the inscription as a public account with a limited point of view. Do not name or reproduce real-world stories, songs, characters, authors, or copied text. Do not invent a tale, audience, origin, lesson, supernatural effect, or sole ownership of identity. Before any passage is selected, re-check the source files, current data schema, narrative continuity, and the owner that would display it. Accept only the smallest coherent set of candidates that can be attributed and reached truthfully.

### Editorial scale notes

**01 — source fidelity.** The anchor remains memorial_storyteller/stories_in_hearts (The Storyteller). Every proposed sentence must stay attached to the cited local record, and every inference must remain visibly an inference. The source can support a human moment without supporting a new mechanic, route, authority, or outcome.

**02 — material attention.** The strongest details in this bank are physical: a threshold, a surface, a line of sight, a document, a marker, a schedule, or a room that still has its old shape. Keep those details ordinary enough to be believed and specific enough to distinguish The Storyteller from another location.

**03 — voice separation.** The three roles in this plan do different work. One notices the immediate object, one protects the record’s limits, and one carries the emotional consequence. Do not let all three speak in the same polished register or give one person the authority of the whole The Storyteller record.

**04 — player space.** The player is allowed to observe before deciding what the observation means. A pause, refusal, question, or incomplete answer can be the passage’s consequence. Do not turn every emotional beat into a prompt, reward, relationship delta, journal flag, or measurable improvement.

**05 — return scale.** A return to The Storyteller should make the earlier passage newly legible, not rewrite it. The later reader may notice a different word, remember a different cost, or decline to repeat the account. The catalog facts remain stable unless a current authorized system says otherwise.

**06 — uncertainty.** Uncertainty is authored content here. It is not a hole that needs to be filled with a hidden culprit, a technical explanation, a secret route, or an invented survivor. When a source leaves a name, cause, or outcome open, the prose can make that openness felt without pretending to solve it.

**07 — dignity.** People associated with The Storyteller should not be reduced to the hazard, policy, machine, or record that surrounds them. Let a person have a practical task, a private limit, a bad day, a memory that is incomplete, or a reason not to answer. Keep the human center larger than the prop.

**08 — content scale.** The 48-beat bank is deliberately editorial. A production owner may select one passage, several passages, or none. The size of this document is a writing resource, not a request to implement every candidate or to create a parallel narrative registry.

**09 — continuity check.** Before placement, compare this bank against newer data, especially records that mention memorial_storyteller/stories_in_hearts or the surrounding region. Confirm names, truth classes, branch outcomes, and display ownership. A matching phrase is not proof that a passage remains current.

**10 — hazard boundary.** The plan’s boundary is explicit: Do not add a physical grave, named survivor, relationship, exact chronology, private thought, or outcome beyond the source. Do not create a quest, flag, menu, reward, relationship delta, route, item, or save state. Treat the inscription as a public account with a limited point of view. Do not name or reproduce real-world stories, songs, characters, authors, or copied text. Do not invent a tale, audience, origin, lesson, supernatural effect, or sole ownership of identity. Keep that limit visible in scene language. A character may be afraid, cautious, angry, or wrong without the prose becoming an operational guide.

**11 — diegetic surface.** A record, notice, conversation, or scene should look like something a survivor could encounter in the existing content owner. It may be a torn page, a spoken recollection, a label, a pause at a threshold, or a later sentence repeated with one word corrected. It should not look like a system specification inside the fiction.

**12 — emotional movement.** The movement through The Storyteller should be felt as a change in attention. The player begins by seeing an object or condition, then hears a person attach meaning to it, then decides whether to carry that meaning forward. Nothing larger is required for the passage to matter.

**13 — open ending.** Close on a human image rather than a verdict. A hand lowers, a page remains folded, a name is repeated once, a room continues its ordinary work, or a landmark stays where it was. The ending should leave room for the player and the current canon to do the next piece of work.

**14 — handoff.** If a content owner accepts a passage, preserve its attribution and keep the owner’s existing state, save, and routing seams. The handoff is complete only when the selected text, its source record, its trigger, and its audience all agree. Otherwise this remains editorial prose for review.

The proposed bank keeps returning to The Storyteller because repetition is part of survival writing. A person sees the same surface twice and notices a different cost. That change belongs to attention, not to an invented state.

A selected passage may mention memorial_storyteller/stories_in_hearts by its display name, by a local nickname already present in the source, or by an ordinary physical detail. Do not add a second identifier merely to make the prose sound larger.

The strongest callback is often a correction. Someone says a word too confidently, another person supplies the source’s narrower wording, and the room continues. That is enough movement for a scene whose subject is memory.

Keep proposed objects portable in meaning but bounded in authority. A page can be carried, a marker can be seen, and a sentence can be repeated without creating an item registry, a universal rule, or a new save section.

Let silence have more than one cause in the prose: respect, fatigue, uncertainty, refusal, distance, or the simple fact that the current record ends. Never use silence as permission to invent a secret.

Where a source names a hazard, write the people’s relationship to that hazard rather than a method for defeating it. Fear, caution, grief, and disagreement are content; operational guidance belongs to an authorized system owner, not this bank.

Where a source names an institution, show its paperwork and its consequences without making the institution omniscient. A form can be current and still miss a person. A schedule can be obeyed and still be unjust.

The handoff question is simple: can a reviewer point from each important claim to a current source and from each proposed passage to an existing consumer? If either answer is no, keep the passage in editorial review.

The document’s length does not widen the canon. It gives the writer room to choose among small moments at The Storyteller while keeping the source boundary visible in every form. A longer bank is useful only when its alternatives remain honest and distinct.

A final reading should sound like a person who has lived with the place, not like a catalog that has learned to emote. Keep the nouns concrete, keep the claims attributed, and let the last sentence leave a little air around the player’s choice.

The last editorial pass should ask whether the passage could belong to someone standing near The Storyteller with limited time, imperfect memory, and no need to impress a reviewer. If the answer is yes, the bank has done its work: it has made a place available for play without pretending the place belongs to the plan.

The plan can end with the ordinary world still in motion: weather, work, a person walking back toward a settlement, or a room waiting for its next reader. The content has consequence because somebody noticed and somebody else chose what to do with that notice.

A clean handoff leaves the source visible, the voice distinct, and the ending quiet enough to be revised if the canon moves.

The anchored place remains a place, not a promise.

Its horizon can hold a sentence and let the next person decide what the sentence means.

That is enough for one honest return.

The record remains open.

The next reader may leave it that way.

## 15. Character and relationship continuity

The proposed voices are roles created for editorial exploration. Do not silently promote them into named survivors, faction leaders, quest givers, permanent settlement residents, operators, patients, or victims. Their relationships remain within each selected passage unless another current source establishes a return. The story can leave a relationship unresolved without creating a hidden standing change.

## 16. Passage-selection map

**Use a scene** when a verified consumer can stage an observable moment without implying unsafe traversal or a technical procedure. **Use a record** when the game already has an appropriate reader-facing content owner and attribution can be preserved. **Use a conversation** when the passage benefits from two people who disagree in good faith. **Use a consequence vignette** only when the existing route exposes the condition that selects it; otherwise use a non-conditional close or omit the variant. These are editorial selection notes, not proposed runtime features.
